No document explicitly corroborates the stated charge comparisons, savings calculations, or the specific platform charge figures asserted for the receiving scheme (Aviva). The switch analysis shows 'Reduction in Yield' figures but does not present them as negligible or justified by risk alignment arguments.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states Quilter platform charges of 0.27%; switch analysis (P2-P14) confirms 0.27% in existing arrangements but does not state negotiated Aviva rate of 0.19% in any document
      - Report states True Potential platform charges of 0.40%; switch analysis confirms 0.40% but does not state Aviva rate of 0.21% in evidence
      - Report states Nest charges of 0.30%; switch analysis confirms 0.30% but does not state Aviva rate of 0.39% in evidence
      - Report claims platform charge savings of £208 for Quilter switch; derived figure at 0.09% difference on Quilter value implies fund value of £231,111, not Quilter's £260,000
      - Report claims True Potential platform charge saving of £88.68 at 0.19% differential; this does not reconcile with stated rates (0.40% - 0.21% = 0.19%, but £88.68 on True Potential value of £46,672.80 implies 0.19% of £46,672.80 = £88.68, which is correct for the differential but the evidence does not state the 0.21% Aviva rate
      - Report claims Nest charge increase is only £19.15 pa (0.09% on £21,272.64); this matches derived figure but the report asserts the new rate is 0.39% versus 0.30% existing, yet the switch analysis shows Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) with no explicit rate stated in the evidence
      - Report asserts True Potential fund charges drop from 0.58% to 0.20%; evidence shows True Potential Schroders Balanced OCF of 1.19% (P15), not 0.58%, and proposed fund documents show True Potential Allianz Balanced, SEI Aggressive, and UBS Aggressive with OCFs of 0.54% to 0.96%, not 0.20%
      - Switch analysis (P8, P10) shows 'Reduction in Yield' of 1.5% to 1.6% when all charges included for Quilter and True Potential; report rationale does not address or explain this material yield reduction
      - Switch analysis (P12) shows +8.97% effect and 0.5% Reduction in Yield for Nest when IAC excluded, but +0.17% effect and 1.5% Reduction in Yield when full charges included (P10); report does not clarify which scenario applies or explain the charge basis used in rationale
    Three existing arrangements each have a stated recommended outcome (FullTransfer) and client-specific rationale is asserted. However, material discrepancies exist between the report's charge comparisons and the evidence. The report states specific savings and rates for the receiving scheme (Aviva) that do not appear in any document in the pack. The Aviva KFD (P1) does not disclose platform charges, fund charges, or total charges; it provides only product information. The switch analysis documents (P2-P14) compare existing arrangements against Aviva but do not state the Aviva charges that underpin the report's savings claims. For True Potential, the report cites 0.58% fund charges as a reason for transfer, but the only True Potential fund document on file (P15) shows OCF of 1.19% for the current Schroders Balanced fund. The fund charge comparison basis is unclear: whether the 0.58% and 0.20% cited refer to the same investment or different fund selections is not established. The switch analysis shows material Reduction in Yield figures (1.5-1.6% for two arrangements, 0.5% for one), which directly contradict the rationale that transfer is justified by cost savings and alignment. The report does not acknowledge or reconcile these yield reductions. The charge calculation discrepancies suggest either (a) the Aviva rates stated in the report are not on file, (b) the comparison excludes certain costs (such as OAC or initial advice charges) in ways not disclosed, or (c) the fund selections and thus the fund charges are different between what the report assumes and what is documented. The rationale references 'lower overall cost structure' and specific savings, but the evidence of those savings is either absent or contradicted by switch analysis showing material yield headwinds. For Nest, the report acknowledges a £19.15 annual increase but does not address the Reduction in Yield shown in the switch analysis. The vulnerability overlay: the client has a stated preference for flexible income (OBJ5) and a growth risk profile (7/10). The meeting summary (P24) confirms risk profile 7. The switch analysis shows three scenarios (all charges, IAC only, no charges), which matters for suitability, but the report does not state which scenario applies to its recommendations or explain the charge treatment. This ambiguity is material to assessing whether the rationale is sound. The client's circumstances (modest retirement income target of £25k-£30k, assets sufficient under stress) and preference for flexibility suggest the risk profile may itself warrant scrutiny, but that is a separate check (CHK-003); here, the issue is that the yield reductions are not explained in the rationale.

  [G6.7] Recommendations that present multiple possible outcomes without stating which is recommended and why
  Potential Concern — severity Low
    Report:   The suitability report presents five options for each of three recommendations (Quilter pension, True Potential pension, Nest pension). The canonical model shows presentsUnresolvedOptions=false for all three recommendations and adviceStatus values of 'Recommended' for four recommendations and 'Suggested' for regular contributions.
    File:     The meeting summaries and fact-find documents evidence that the client's objectives include consolidating pension pots for better management, exploring tax-efficient strategies, and understanding options such as flexi-access drawdown and annuities [P3]. Meeting notes from 18/03/2026 indicate the client's risk profile was assessed as level seven (growth-oriented) and investment preferences were discussed [P1, P4]. The client was noted to have 'no ties or preference to leave the funds with Quilter' [quoted in report rationale].     Discrepancies:

The file evidence supports the client's objective to consolidate but does not document consideration or rejection of these specific alternatives.

      - The report recommends a specific investment allocation (50% HSBC Global Strategy Dynamic Portfolio C Acc and 50% Vanguard LifeStrategy 80% Equity) but the meeting summaries do not evidence explicit client agreement to this specific allocation mix or consideration of alternative allocations within the chosen platform.
    The report clearly marks one option (Option 5: Aviva Platform) as recommended and the others as rejected, satisfying the primary requirement that a single option is identified as recommended. The canonical model field presentsUnresolvedOptions is set to false, indicating the extraction found no unresolved options. However, the requirement's true substance is whether the rationale for the recommended option is supported and whether rejected alternatives are explained. The file evidence supports that the client seeks consolidation and has no preference for Quilter, but does not contain explicit documented discussion of why Options 1-4 were unsuitable. The investment allocation (50/50 split) is stated in the report but no file evidence shows how this specific mix was chosen or whether alternatives were considered. Under the guard that a client decision recorded in a file note resolves an option the report leaves open, the file notes do not contain explicit written client instructions accepting the Aviva consolidation or the specific allocation, only objectives that the Aviva recommendation would satisfy. The report itself is internally consistent (options presented with one marked recommended) but the supporting documentation for why rejected options are unsuitable is not evident in the file evidence provided.

  [G6.8] Recommendations state what action will be taken, when, and by whom where that is material to suitability
  Potential Concern — severity Low — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report states that transfers will be arranged by the adviser (responsible party identified as 'Adviser' for transfer recommendations). An expected timescale of '10-15 working days' is provided for administration, disinvestment and banking processes. Initial advice fee of 3% (£9,838.36) will be paid directly by the pension scheme on transfer. Ongoing advice charges of 1% per annum (circa £3,181.08 for first year) will be paid via Aviva Platform. Cancellation rights of 30 days are stated. Out-of-market risk is disclosed. The report states 'Morgan Seddon' should 'Document the advice report and process disinvestment and payment within four to six weeks' according to the meeting summary.
    File:     Meeting Summary [P1] records that 'Morgan Seddon' will 'Document the advice report and process disinvestment and payment within four to six weeks.' This aligns with adviser responsibility but specifies a longer timeframe (four to six weeks) than the suitability report's stated '10-15 working days' for withdrawal access, though these may refer to different phases of the process. The meeting summary identifies Morgan Seddon by name as the responsible party. No other documents provide conflicting information on who will take action or when.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states '10-15 working days' for 'Should you require access to these funds in the future'; meeting summary states 'four to six weeks' for 'process disinvestment and payment'. These appear to address different operational phases: the former withdrawal access timescale, the latter implementation timescale for the switch itself.
      - Report identifies responsible party as 'Adviser' in structured data; meeting summary names the specific individual 'Morgan Seddon'.
    The first discrepancy reflects different time horizons: the report's '10-15 working days' addresses future withdrawal access timescales (stated in the context 'Should you require access to these funds in the future'), while the meeting summary's 'four to six weeks' addresses the initial implementation timeline for processing the disinvestment and payment related to the pension transfers. These are not contradictory but sequential phases and no guard explains this away — the distinction is material to suitability insofar as the client needs to understand both when the switch will complete and how quickly she can later access funds. The report does state this implementation timescale indirectly through the meeting summary but does not include it in the suitability report itself. The second discrepancy—'Adviser' versus 'Morgan Seddon'—is explained by the guard that naming the adviser firm satisfies 'by whom'; the meeting summary provides the individual's name while the report's canonical structure records the role. The report adequately identifies action (transfers to be arranged), by whom (the adviser), and provides a timescale for future access (10-15 working days). However, the four-to-six-week implementation timescale for the switch itself, which is material to suitability (it determines when the client's pension enters the new arrangement and begins accruing the recommended structure), is stated in an external document (the meeting summary) and not clearly restated in the suitability report's implementation section. The report does disclose initial and ongoing fees, cancellation rights, and out-of-market risk. The withdrawal access timescale is stated as applying to 'Should you require access to these funds in the future', and the report notes the client is comfortable with this because she has liquid cash available. No contradiction is found between the report and evidence on the specific responsibilities or action to be taken; the timing discrepancy relates to different operational phases rather than a contradiction about when the recommendation will be implemented.

CHK-007 — Costs, Charges and Value of the Recommendation
Outcome: Potential Concern

  Potential Concern across 11 requirement(s). 10 raised a concern (G7.1, G7.3, G7.4, G7.5, G7.6, G7.7, G7.8, G7.9, G7.10, G7.11). Report states ongoing Investment Management charge of 0.20% pa for recommended solution; Aviva illustration [P19] shows Weighted Average investment charge of 0.20% for HSBC and Vanguard funds combined, matching the report's figure. Report's total ongoing charge of 0.39% (0.19% + 0.20%) is corroborated by Aviva illustration platform and investment charges combining to 0.39%. Report excludes transaction costs of underlying funds with stated reasons; Aviva illustration [P18] notes 'Product and Investment charges' separately from 'all charges' including adviser fees, confirming transaction costs distinction. 11 cited quote(s) could not be found in the evidence supplied and were rejected.

  [G7.1] All material costs disclosed — product, platform, fund, transaction, advice
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report discloses: Platform charges 0.19% pa, Investment Management charges 0.20% pa, totalling 0.39% pa ongoing charges. Ongoing annual adviser charge of 1.00% considered as part of new solution projections. Transaction costs of underlying funds are excluded as variable and subject to change based on market conditions and trading activity, though accounted for in fund performance figures net of all charges. Existing advice charges applicable to True Potential plan are included in comparison. The report states the solution is more cost-effective than a typical stakeholder pension.

    Discrepancies:
      - Report states ongoing Investment Management charge of 0.20% pa for recommended solution; Aviva illustration [P19] shows Weighted Average investment charge of 0.20% for HSBC and Vanguard funds combined, matching the report's figure
      - Report's total ongoing charge of 0.39% (0.19% + 0.20%) is corroborated by Aviva illustration platform and investment charges combining to 0.39%
      - Report excludes transaction costs of underlying funds with stated reasons; Aviva illustration [P18] notes 'Product and Investment charges' separately from 'all charges' including adviser fees, confirming transaction costs distinction
      - Report includes initial adviser charge of 3% and ongoing adviser charge of 1.00% in projections; Critical Yield section [P9] confirms these figures are applied to comparison analysis
      - Monetary charge calculation: Report states £1,278.99 total annual recurring charges; derived figure from canonical model confirms this is 0.39% of £327,945.44 (total arrangements), which equals £1,278.79, a rounding difference of £0.20
      - Report states True Potential existing arrangement charges are included in analysis; however, the replacement analysis tables [P8] show current annual recurring charge for True Potential as 0.98%, but canonical model extraction shows existing True Potential has three components: Platform 0.40%, Ongoing Advice 0.50%, Fund Management 0.58%, totalling 1.48%, not 0.98%
      - Report's switching analysis comparison [P8] shows Nest 'Current Annual Recurring Charge Impact' as 0.09% difference (£19.15), but does not integrate the stated 1.8% contribution charge from Nest existing arrangements
      - Aviva illustration [P18] shows cumulative impact table with 'After taking all charges' column reducing fund from £372,000 to £337,000 over full term, representing 2.2% reduction in growth rate; report does not provide equivalent aggregated cumulative impact figure in the costs section itself
    The report discloses platform and investment management charges for the recommended solution (0.19% and 0.20% respectively, totalling 0.39% pa) and these are corroborated by Aviva's Key Features Document and illustration. The monetary equivalent of £1,278.99 pa reconciles arithmetically with the total arrangement value of £327,945.44 at the stated 0.39% rate (rounding difference immaterial at £0.20). The initial adviser fee of 3% and ongoing adviser fee of 1.00% are both stated and applied in the Critical Yield comparison analysis, with the 3% deducted from new solution on day one and 1.00% ongoing reflected in projections. Transaction costs are explicitly excluded with clear stated reasons (variability, accounted for in net fund performance figures), which meets the disclosure obligation for exclusions. However, material discrepancies emerge on the existing arrangement charges used in the switching analysis. The report's replacement analysis table shows True Potential's 'Current Annual Recurring Charge' as 0.98%, but the canonical extraction identifies existing True Potential charges as three components (0.40% platform + 0.50% ongoing advice + 0.58% fund = 1.48% total). This discrepancy of 0.50% (the ongoing advice charge) materially affects the claimed cost benefit of switching, as the switching analysis does not account for the adviser's existing 0.50% ongoing charge being replaced by the new 1.00% adviser charge, a net increase of 0.50% requiring justification. Similarly, Nest's existing arrangement charges are listed as both 0.30% plan charge and 1.8% contribution charge, but the switching comparison uses only 0.09% as the impact difference, which appears to exclude the 1.8% contribution charge without explanation or integration. The cumulative effect illustration exists in the Aviva provider illustration [P18] showing charge impact of 2.2% reduction in growth rate over the full term, but this aggregated view is not replicated in the report's own costs section; instead, the report presents only the percentage and monetary figures without showing the cumulative effect of charges on projected fund values in a comparable format to the provider illustration. The report does not provide a detailed reconciliation or explanation of these charge changes between existing and recommended arrangements, nor does it explicitly address whether the benefits of the recommended solution justify the net increase in adviser charges and any other cost changes. The value statement that the solution is 'more cost effective than a typical stakeholder pension' [P12] is not supported by a direct comparison of the recommended charges against stakeholder pension charges, which are not disclosed in the case file. No documentary evidence shows that a benefit analysis of the recommendation against the charge increase has been prepared or disclosed.

  [G7.3] Cumulative impact of costs and charges
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report states annual recurring charges for the recommended Aviva solution as Platform 0.19%, Investment Management 0.20%, Total 0.39%, equating to £1,278.99 annually. It presents a Reduction in Yield (RIY) of 1.5% to 1.6% depending on growth rate assumption, with Critical Yield rates of 0.41% (low growth), 3.37% (mid growth), and 6.32% (high growth). The effect of switching is stated as -12.42% reduction in maturity value. An initial adviser charge of 3% is deducted from the new solution, with ongoing annual adviser charge of 1.00%. The report states the recommended plan requires a rate of return of 0.41% to 6.32% to match existing schemes, with real-terms projected values at age 87 of £58,200 (0% growth), £146,000 (2.94% growth), and £359,000 (5.88% growth). Transaction costs of underlying funds are excluded on the basis they are variable and already reflected in performance figures shown net of all charges.
    File:     Aviva platform charge is confirmed as 0.19% in the illustration [P21]. Investment fund charges are confirmed: HSBC Global Strategy Dynamic Portfolio 0.19% and Vanguard LifeStrategy 80% Equity 0.20%, with weighted average 0.20% [P21]. The Aviva Pension Terms & Conditions [P3, P4] state 'There are no additional Aviva charges levied for switching. There may be transactional costs where applicable.' The Key Features Document [P21] confirms the Aviva charge of 0.19% and investment charges as stated. Real-terms projections at age 87 from SAP illustrations [P22, P24] show: Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) £58,200 (0%), £146,000 (2.94%), £359,000 (5.88%) with RIY of 0.5%. Existing schemes projected at £53,497.38 (0%), £134,612.81 (2.94%), £330,027.33 (5.88%) with no RIY stated. The illustration [P21] states initial fund charge will not be taken on transfer as it will have been paid previously. Vanguard & HSBC fact sheets [P8] confirm fund OCF charges at 0.20% and 0.19% respectively. No evidence in the file supports or quantifies the 1.00% ongoing adviser charge, the 3% initial adviser charge, or the specific Critical Yield figures of 0.41%, 3.37%, 6.32%.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report asserts RIY of 1.5% to 1.6%; SAP illustration [P24] shows RIY of 0.5% for recommended Aviva solution—a 1.0 to 1.1 percentage point discrepancy
      - Report states Critical Yield rates of 0.41%, 3.37%, 6.32%; these figures do not appear in any supporting document and cannot be reconciled to fund charges or growth assumptions
      - Report states effect of switching is -12.42% reduction in maturity value; this figure does not appear in fund documentation, illustrations or supporting evidence
      - Report claims ongoing adviser charge of 1.00%; no adviser agreement, client agreement or fee schedule in the file supports this figure
      - Report claims initial adviser charge of 3%; no document in the file confirms or authorises this charge
      - Report projects maturity values at age 87 as £58,200, £146,000, £359,000 (0%, 2.94%, 5.88%); SAP illustration [P24] confirms these identical figures, but the illustration basis (real-terms, with or without adviser charges included) is not explicitly aligned in the report
      - Existing arrangements comparison table [P18] shows True Potential 0.98% recurring charge, but this excludes the stated 0.50% ongoing advice charge from existing arrangements, creating ambiguity about whether adviser charges are consistently applied to both old and new solutions
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: No client agreement or fee schedule detailing the 1.00% ongoing adviser charge or 3% initial adviser charge, No explanation of the basis on which Critical Yield rates (0.41%, 3.37%, 6.32%) were calculated or what assumptions underlie them, No reconciliation between the report's stated RIY of 1.5–1.6% and the SAP illustration's RIY of 0.5%, No document explaining the -12.42% 'effect of switching' figure or its calculation method, No detail on whether adviser charges are included in the projected maturity values (£58,200, £146,000, £359,000) or excluded, No explanation of whether the 1.00% ongoing adviser charge is in addition to or included within the stated 0.39% total platform and investment management charges, No detail on how transaction costs, stated as excluded, were estimated or their typical magnitude for the recommended funds, No comparison of adviser charges between existing arrangements (True Potential noted as having 0.50% ongoing advice charge) and new solution to establish whether the 1.00% represents an increase or change in adviser remuneration
    The report asserts specific cost and yield figures that are material to demonstrating suitability but lacks supporting evidence for critical components. The platform charge (0.19%) and fund charges (0.20% weighted average) are corroborated by fund documentation and the Aviva illustration. However, three significant discrepancies emerge: (1) RIY mismatch: the report states 1.5–1.6% RIY but the SAP illustration [P24] shows 0.5% RIY for the recommended Aviva solution. RIY directly measures the cumulative impact of charges on growth, so this 1.0–1.1 percentage point gap is material and unexplained. (2) Adviser charges unsupported: the report claims a 1.00% ongoing adviser charge and 3% initial adviser charge, but no fee agreement or client agreement in the file substantiates these figures. The report states these are 'included as part of the new solution' in the Critical Yield appendix [P19], yet the client agreement and adviser fee terms are not provided. (3) Critical Yield figures absent from evidence: the report presents Critical Yield rates of 0.41%, 3.37%, 6.32% as key metrics of the switching decision, but these do not appear in any illustration, fund document or calculation method in the file. The appendix states these include adviser charges but provides no transparent derivation. (4) Effect of switching unexplained: the -12.42% figure is presented as 'the effect this will have on the fund if moved to Aviva' but no calculation, illustration or document explains how this was derived or what it represents. (5) Inconsistent adviser charge treatment: the existing arrangements table shows True Potential with 0.98% recurring charge but the report notes a separate 0.50% ongoing advice charge; it is unclear whether the new solution's 1.00% adviser charge is comparable or constitutes an increase. (6) Basis of projections unclear: maturity values match the SAP illustration exactly, but the report does not explicitly state whether these are real-terms or nominal, and whether adviser charges are reflected in or additional to these figures. The guard that illustration growth rates differ from cashflow assumptions does not resolve this, as the RIY discrepancy appears on the illustration itself. The cumulative effect of charges—the core requirement of [G7.3]—is never explicitly shown as a single aggregated figure or as a comparison of cumulative cost impact between existing and recommended arrangements over the retirement term. The exclusion of transaction costs is stated but not quantified, and no evidence supports the assertion that they are 'already accounted for in the fund's performance figures.' Comparison cannot be performed in full because the adviser fee structure, its interaction with platform and fund charges, and the basis of Critical Yield cannot be verified against the file.

  [G7.4] Cost comparison with alternative options
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report presents a cost comparison table for two existing arrangements (EA2 True Potential and EA3 Nest) showing: EA2 existing annual charge 0.98% (£457.39), impact -0.59% (-£275.37); EA3 existing annual charge 0.30% (£63.82), impact +0.09% (+£19.15). The report also states EA1 (Quilter) platform charges of 0.27% representing £208 savings per annum. The new Aviva solution is stated as 0.39% total (£1,278.99 annually). The basis is stated as 'IncludingAdviceFees' for the comparison.
    File:     Provider documentation [P16] confirms True Potential charges: Platform 0.40%, Ongoing Advice 0.50%, Weighted Average Fund Management 0.58%, totalling 1.48%. This is the existing arrangement for EA2. The Nest existing arrangement documentation [P16] confirms Plan Charges 0.30% and Contribution charge 1.8%. The Aviva illustrations [P1], [P2], [P3] provide SAP comparisons but do not independently restate the comparison figures. Fund literature [P12], [P15], [P18], [P22] provides OCF figures (0.76%, 0.63%, 0.87%, 0.54%) but these are underlying fund charges, not platform totals. The comparison table in [P4] shows the same figures: True Potential 0.98%, Nest 0.30%, Aviva solution 0.39%.
    Discrepancies:
      - EA2 (True Potential) existing annual charge stated as 0.98% in comparison table [P4] but provider documentation [P16] shows component charges of 0.40% + 0.50% + 0.58% = 1.48%. The 0.98% figure excludes the ongoing advice charge of 0.50%.
      - EA3 (Nest) existing annual charge stated as 0.30% in comparison table [P4], which matches plan charges component, but provider documentation [P16] shows a separate contribution charge of 1.8%. The comparison excludes the contribution charge.
      - The monetary figure for EA2 existing charge (£457.39) at 0.98% implies a fund value of £46,672.45, which matches EA2's transfer value of £46,672.80 (within rounding). However, this calculation appears to exclude the 0.50% ongoing advice charge that is documented in [P16].
      - The monetary figure for EA3 existing charge (£63.82) at 0.30% implies a fund value of £21,273.33, which matches EA3's transfer value of £21,272.64 (within rounding). However, the 1.8% contribution charge is not included.
      - EA1 (Quilter) cost saving stated as £208 per annum at 0.27% implies a fund value of £77,037.04, which does not match any single arrangement value or the total of all three arrangements (£327,945.44). The stated percentage and amount do not reconcile to any identified asset base in the report.
      - The comparison basis is stated as 'IncludingAdviceFees' but the EA2 existing charge of 0.98% appears to exclude the documented ongoing advice charge of 0.50%, contradicting the stated basis.
      - Report page 6 states conditionally 'If you were to retain the pension with Quilter the platform charges would be 0.27%' but this is conditional language whereas the comparison table presents it as actual. The three arrangements being compared have different charge compositions: Quilter conditional/hypothetical, True Potential actual documented charges, Nest actual documented charges.
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Clarification of which charge components are included in the 0.98% figure for True Potential — specifically whether the 0.50% ongoing advice charge is intentionally excluded and on what basis., Clarification of which charge components are included in the 0.30% figure for Nest — specifically whether the 1.8% contribution charge is intentionally excluded and the rationale., The basis for the £208 annual saving on Quilter (0.27%) — what plan value this is calculated on, as it does not match the stated Quilter fund value or any documented asset base., Reconciliation of the comparison basis 'IncludingAdviceFees' against the actual composition of charges used in each row of the comparison table., Whether the Aviva 0.39% figure (Platform 0.19% + Investment Management 0.20% = 0.39%) is calculated on the same basis as the existing arrangement figures, or whether it also excludes any material cost layer.
    The report asserts that costs are being compared on a basis 'IncludingAdviceFees', but the evidence shows material discrepancies in the charge composition across arrangements. For EA2 (True Potential), the documented existing charges total 1.48% (0.40% + 0.50% + 0.58%), but the comparison uses 0.98%. The 0.50% ongoing advice charge is documented in [P16] but absent from the comparison figure. For EA3 (Nest), the documented charges include a 1.8% contribution charge [P16], but the comparison uses only the 0.30% plan charges. These exclusions are not explained. The report derives monetary amounts from the stated percentages and fund values, and those calculations are arithmetically sound (457.39 = 46,672.80 × 0.98%; 63.82 = 21,272.64 × 0.30%), but they are based on incomplete charge figures relative to the provider documentation. For EA1 (Quilter), the stated saving of £208 at 0.27% implies a fund value of £77,037, which is unexplained — it is neither the Quilter fund value (£260,000) nor any component of the total. This figure appears to be an error or requires clarification of a methodology not disclosed. The comparison table [P4] presents EA2 and EA3 with stated 'impact' figures (the change from existing to proposed), but without visibility of the complete existing charge structure, the sufficiency of the comparison to support suitability cannot be established. The stated basis 'IncludingAdviceFees' cannot be verified against the figures used. The report states that the new solution (Aviva) costs 0.39% [P4] and is presented as superior on cost grounds, but the comparison is not on a like-for-like basis if the existing arrangements' figures are incomplete. No aggregated all-in cost or cumulative impact illustration is provided in the evidence. The critical yield analysis in [P10] acknowledges that the Aviva plan 'is more expensive than your current True Potential plan' on an advice-fee basis, which contradicts the direction of the 'impact' shown in the comparison table (-0.59% cost saving for EA2). This internal contradiction undermines the reliability of the comparison.

  [G7.5] Benefit versus cost — fair value
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   The suitability report asserts multiple cost benefits: (1) 'Lower overall cost structure compared to the Quilter plan' [P1]; (2) Platform charges reduced from 0.27% to 0.19%, saving £208 per annum based on estimated fund value [P1]; (3) For True Potential: platform charges reduced from 0.40% to 0.21%, saving £88.68 per annum [P1]; (4) For True Potential: fund charges reduced from 0.58% to 0.20%, saving £177.35 per annum [P1]; (5) Benefits cited include active monitoring, fund alignment with risk level, wider investment options, flexible retirement options, and online platform access [P1]. The report states the CIP approach offers 'Cost Efficiency' balancing performance and cost while optimising value [P3]. No aggregated all-in-cost comparison is presented in the report. The report does not disclose the ongoing adviser charges (0.50% for True Potential per the extracted inconsistencies) in the cost comparison tables. The true cost comparison excludes the adviser's ongoing remuneration from the switching analysis, presenting only platform and fund charges as the basis for the cost-savings claim.
    File:     Evidence shows existing arrangements total £327,945.44 across three pensions (Quilter £260,000.00 at 0.27% platform charge = £208 annual; True Potential £46,672.80 with 0.40% platform + 0.50% ongoing advice + 0.58% fund charges totalling 1.48%; Nest £21,272.64 at 0.30% plan charge + 1.8% contribution charge = 2.1% total). Switching analysis documents [P12, P14, P15, P16] show projected fund values at retirement (age 87): Aviva Pension Portfolio at 2.94% growth projects to £151,000 (inflation-adjusted) versus existing plan £134,612.81; RIY shown as 0.4% [P14]. The arithmetic check from the canonical model reveals: EA1 (Quilter) annual charge £208 at 0.27% implies fund value £77,037.04, which matches no single arrangement; Solution total ongoing £1,278.99 at 0.39% implies fund value £327,946.15 (matches total of all arrangements); EA2 (True Potential) existing annual charge £457.39 at 0.98% implies value £46,672.45 (matches True Potential value); EA3 (Nest) existing annual charge £63.82 at 0.30% implies value £21,273.33 (matches Nest value). The report claims platform charges for Quilter will reduce to 0.19% but provides no independent documentation confirming the receiving scheme's platform charges. Fund literature shows True Potential funds with OCF charges (e.g., Goldman Sachs Balanced at 0.87% [P5]). Proposed Aviva funds show ongoing costs of 0.2154% weighted average [P11].
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states platform charge saving of £208 per annum for Quilter based on reducing from 0.27% to 0.19%, but the £208 figure at 0.27% implies a fund value of £77,037.04, not the £260,000 Quilter value stated in existing arrangements
      - Report claims True Potential platform charge saving of £88.68 (from 0.40% to 0.21%), but £88.68 at 0.40% implies a fund value of £22,170, not the £46,672.80 stated for True Potential
      - Report claims True Potential fund charge saving of £177.35 (from 0.58% to 0.20%), but £177.35 at 0.58% implies a fund value of £30,577, not £46,672.80
      - True Potential existing charges stated in arrangements table as 0.40% + 0.50% + 0.58% = 1.48%, but replacement analysis shows current recurring charge as 0.98%, excluding the 0.50% ongoing advice charge from the switching comparison
      - Report does not disclose that it is excluding the 0.50% ongoing adviser charge from True Potential when presenting cost comparisons, creating a material omission in the 'all-in' cost analysis
      - Report claims £208 saving from Quilter switch, which at stated 0.27% rate would apply to a fund value of £77,037, but Quilter value is £260,000; if applied correctly to £260,000 at 0.08% reduction (0.27% to 0.19%), the saving would be £208, but this basis is not stated
      - Nest existing charges listed as two components (0.30% plan + 1.8% contribution) totalling 2.1%, but switching analysis uses only 0.30% in comparisons, silently excluding the 1.8% contribution charge
      - Report does not specify the precise ongoing fund charges for the proposed Aviva arrangement in the main body; literature shows weighted average of 0.2154% but report's stated 0.19% platform charge is not independently corroborated
      - Report states benefits including 'active monitoring and adjust investments as required' but does not disclose the cost of this ongoing service or whether it is included in the 0.19%/0.21% platform charges quoted
      - RIY of 0.4% shown in switching analysis [P14] is significantly lower than the claimed cost savings, suggesting the projections do not fully account for all stated benefit claims
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Independent provider illustration or charges schedule confirming the receiving scheme (Aviva) platform charge of 0.19% and total ongoing charges, Confirmation of whether the 0.19% platform charge includes or excludes ongoing adviser remuneration, Explicit disclosure of all ongoing costs in the receiving scheme, including adviser fees, Breakdown of total annual charges (platform + fund + advice) for the recommended arrangement versus each existing arrangement on a consistent basis across all three pensions, Current performance data or RIY for Quilter arrangement to validate whether switching cost savings are material relative to performance differences, Confirmation of what 'estimated fund value' the £208 saving calculation is based on, and whether this differs from the stated Quilter value of £260,000
    The report claims material cost savings but the arithmetic does not reconcile. (1) Quilter: Report states £208 saving from 0.27% to 0.19%, representing 0.08% reduction. At the stated Quilter value of £260,000, a 0.08% saving would be £208, which is arithmetically correct. However, the report does not state this basis explicitly, and the canonical model's working appears to compute £208 at 0.27% against a fund value of £77,037, suggesting either the £208 figure or the fund value is drawn from a different calculation. The true test is whether £208 ÷ £260,000 = 0.08%, which it does, so the saving is plausible IF applied to the full Quilter value, but the report's language ('based on the estimated fund value') creates ambiguity about which fund value is in scope. (2) True Potential: Report claims £88.68 saving (platform) and £177.35 (funds), totalling £265.03. The report states these are 'based on the current value of the fund' (singular), which is £46,672.80. Check: £46,672.80 × 0.19% = £88.68 ✓ (0.40% − 0.21% = 0.19%). But the report states platform charges reduce from 0.40% to 0.21%, a reduction of 0.19 percentage points, not 0.19% of the fund. This is a material mislabeling of the benefit. Fund charge saving: £46,672.80 × (0.58% − 0.20%) = £177.35 ✓. However, the critical issue is that the report's switching analysis in [P12] omits the 0.50% ongoing adviser charge that is listed in the existing arrangements table as part of True Potential's total 1.48% annual charge. When the switching analysis is performed, it shows only 0.98% as the 'current annual recurring charge', which is 0.40% + 0.58% but excludes 0.50% advice. This is a silent omission of a material cost layer from one side of the comparison. The receiving scheme analysis [P11] shows Aviva ongoing costs of 0.2154% (fund charges only), with no explicit ongoing adviser charge stated. If adviser charges are continuing at 0.50%, the true cost comparison is: True Potential 1.48% vs. Aviva 0.2154% + 0.50% (if ongoing advice continues) = 0.7154%, a saving of only 0.76 percentage points, not the 0.98% − 0.2154% = 0.7646% implied by the switching analysis. (3) Nest: The existing arrangements table lists two charges: 0.30% (plan) and 1.8% (contribution), but the switching analysis [P12] uses only 0.30% for comparison. The 1.8% contribution charge is noted separately but not integrated into the all-in cost comparison. This is a material omission if the contribution charge continues post-switch. (4) Aggregation: The report does not present a single all-in-cost table showing total annual charges for existing arrangements (all three combined) versus the recommended arrangement. The canonical model shows 'Solution total ongoing' of £1,278.99 at 0.39%, which would imply a blended rate across £327,945 total assets. The report does not disclose this figure or explain how ongoing costs scale. (5) Benefits justification: The report cites benefits including 'active monitoring', 'tailored investment strategy', 'flexible retirement options' and 'online platform', but does not quantify these or explain how they justify costs where they are higher than the passive alternatives. The comparison between Aviva and investment pathways [P1, P3] notes investment pathways have 'potentially lower costs' and 'less requirement for ongoing advice', but the report does not disclose whether Aviva ongoing charges include advice or are platform-only. (6) Guard application: The instruction notes that a comparison may be unsafe where bases differ. Here, the comparison of existing charges includes components (e.g., contribution charges, ongoing advice) that are not transparently mirrored in the receiving scheme, making the comparison unsafe. An assumption that adviser charges continue at 0.50% on Aviva would materially change the cost conclusion, but this assumption is never stated. (7) RIY: The switching analysis shows RIY of 0.4% [P14], which is the annual yield required from the existing scheme to match the projected retirement value of the recommended scheme. This is significantly lower than the claimed cost savings of 0.76% to 0.98%, which suggests either the growth assumptions used in the projection are conservative, or the cost benefit is smaller than claimed. The report does not reconcile the RIY outcome with the cost-savings claims.

        "We have negotiated a reduced platform charges of 0.19%, compared to the Quilter charges of 0.27%, representing a savings of £208 per annum, based on the estimated fund value."

  [G7.6] Costs are excessive relative to the benefits provided
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   The suitability report states that the proposed plan will need to outperform the current plan by 1.23% per annum to age 89. This figure is presented as the annual outperformance required for a Nest comparison including adviser fees. The report asserts this as a basis for evaluating whether higher costs deliver sufficient benefit.
    File:     No supporting document outside the suitability report itself is retrieved to corroborate, contradict or evidence this outperformance requirement. The requirement is assessed from the report alone, as it compares the report against itself. The extracted model shows the 1.23% figure is stated at page 18 of the suitability report.
    This requirement tests whether costs are excessive relative to benefits. The report asserts a single outperformance metric (1.23% per annum) as the basis on which costs justify themselves: if the recommended arrangement outperforms the current one by this margin, the higher costs are offset by superior returns. The extracted canonical model shows this figure is stated in the report. The internal consistency report notes that True Potential's charges are stated as 0.40% platform + 0.50% ongoing advice + 0.58% fund management = 1.48%, whilst Nest shows 0.30% plan charges and 1.8% contribution charge. The difference in total annual recurring charges between arrangements is material. The 1.23% outperformance threshold appears designed to offset higher costs in the solution relative to at least one existing arrangement. However, the report does not present a full cost comparison table showing existing vs. recommended charges side by side with the outperformance requirement mapped to each. The report does state the outperformance figure and ties it explicitly to adviser fees, indicating the adviser has quantified the cost barrier. Without a side-by-side comparison showing which arrangement's charges this threshold is derived from, the basis of the comparison cannot be fully validated. The charge arithmetic derived from the canonical model shows: EA2 (True Potential) existing annual charge £457.39 at 0.98%, and proposed solution total ongoing £1,278.99 at 0.39%. The internal inconsistency report flags that the 0.98% figure in replacement analysis does not match the 1.48% sum of stated charges, and that the 1.8% contribution charge for Nest is excluded from the replacement analysis switching assessment. This suggests the outperformance calculation may be incomplete or based on partial charge figures. The report does not disclose a full aggregated all-in cost comparison, nor does it show projected values under baseline vs. recommended scenarios to demonstrate that the outperformance would materially occur. The 1.23% threshold is stated but not reconciled to specific charge differences between the current and proposed arrangements. No evidence is provided of the service proposition or benefits attached to the additional costs.
      · Suitability report 2026.md [I_RecommendationsAndAdvice] (I_page18)
        "your proposed plan will need to outperform your current plan by 1.23% per annum to age 89"

  [G7.7] Alternatives provide better cost or benefit to the client
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report recommends switching to Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) from existing arrangements (Quilter £260,000, True Potential £46,672.80, Nest £21,272.64). The report states: 'The receiving scheme has a lower overall cost structure compared to the Quilter plan. We have negotiated a reduced platform charges of 0.19%, compared to the Quilter charges of 0.27%, representing a savings of £208 per annum.' For Nest, the report states the Aviva platform 'will result in lower annual charges at 0.39%' compared to Nest's existing charges. The report recommends the multi-asset fund 'provides a professionally managed, diversified investment solution that aligns with your financial goals and risk profile' and offers 'simplicity, cost-efficiency, and flexibility.' The recommendations reference cost savings against specific alternatives but do not provide a comprehensive comparative cost analysis table or aggregated all-in cost comparison.
    File:     The switching analysis documents [P1], [P3], [P5], [P7] provide switching analysis reports comparing projected fund values at retirement across multiple growth scenarios (0%, 2.94%, 5.88%). For example, at 2.94% growth: existing schemes project £134,612.81 (no charges scenario [P7]) vs Aviva £151,000. These documents show 'Reduction in Yield' calculations ranging from 0.3% to 0.4% across scenarios, and critical yield rates required from Aviva (e.g., 3.03% vs 2.94% assumed at mid growth [P14]). The documents note different charge treatments: [P1] 'All charges inc existing OAC', [P3] 'All charges ex existing OAC', [P5] 'IAC only ex existing OAC', [P7] 'no charges'. The suitability report [P9], [P10] states savings of £208 per annum on Quilter platform charges (0.19% vs 0.27%) and references switching into Aviva, but does not cross-reference the detailed switching analyses. The report identifies True Potential and Nest as switching targets but provides no comparable quantified cost basis for these against the savings calculation method used for Quilter.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report quantifies the Quilter switching benefit as '£208 per annum, based on the estimated fund value' but does not state the fund value this calculation is based on. The derived figure analysis indicates £208 at 0.27% implies £77,037.04, which matches neither Quilter's £260,000 nor any combination of the three arrangements.
      - The switching analyses show multiple charge treatment scenarios (with existing OAC, without existing OAC, IAC only) but the report's recommendation does not specify which scenario applies or whether all three were considered in reaching the recommendation.
      - For True Potential, the report states the Aviva platform will be 'lower overall cost structure' but provides no equivalent quantified saving (unlike the £208 stated for Quilter). The switching analysis documents show True Potential existing annual charge of 0.98% [P14] but the report does not reference or reconcile this.
      - For Nest, the report claims Aviva's 0.39% charge is lower, but the existing arrangements table shows Nest with Plan Charges 0.30% plus Contribution charge 1.8%, totalling 2.1%. The 0.39% comparison appears to exclude the contribution charge from Nest.
      - The report asserts the recommendation provides 'simplicity, cost-efficiency, and flexibility' as benefits but does not quantify the cost-efficiency benefit or demonstrate it exceeds alternatives in measurable terms.
      - Death benefit comparison [P12], [P13] shows Aviva provides 97% of existing plan's death benefit (£65,907 vs £67,945), a shortfall of approximately £2,038. The report does not address whether this reduction is acceptable or whether the cost savings justify the loss.
      - The switching analyses project fund values under no-charges scenario [P7] showing Existing Scheme £134,612.81 vs Aviva £151,000 at 2.94% growth, but when all charges are included [P3], True Potential projects £85,961 vs switching analysis projections differ. The report does not reconcile these outcomes or explain which charge scenario applies to the final recommendation.
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: The fund value basis for the £208 per annum Quilter saving calculation is not stated in the report. Derived analysis suggests £77,037.04, which does not match any single arrangement or documented total., Quantified ongoing annual cost comparison for True Potential is absent; the report states 'lower overall cost structure' without a pound amount or percentage basis., Quantified cost comparison for Nest is absent; the report claims 0.39% is lower without reconciling against the stated Plan Charges 0.30% plus Contribution charge 1.8%., Aggregated all-in cost (initial plus ongoing) is not provided in the report for any of the three existing arrangements or the recommended Aviva arrangement., The report does not specify which of the four switching analysis scenarios (all charges with OAC, all charges without OAC, IAC only, no charges) was used to support the final recommendation., The relationship between the switching analysis documents and the suitability report recommendation is not explained; it is unclear whether the analyses informed the recommendation or were produced separately., No comparative alternatives considered analysis is present in the canonical model; the report recommends Aviva but does not disclose what alternatives were considered at the same cost level or with similar benefits.
    The requirement to show that alternatives provide better cost or benefit is not met by the evidence available. The report makes three separate switching recommendations (Quilter, True Potential, Nest to Aviva) on cost grounds but substantiates only one with a specific figure: £208 per annum for Quilter. This figure cannot be reconciled with the documented fund value (£260,000), as derived analysis shows £208 at the stated 0.27% charge implies £77,037.04. The discrepancy is material and unexplained. For True Potential and Nest, the report asserts cost benefits without quantification, making verification impossible. The switching analysis documents [P1]–[P7] provide detailed projections across multiple charge scenarios and growth rates, but the report does not reference them or specify which scenario applies. This creates internal inconsistency: the report recommends action based on cost but does not tie the recommendation to the supporting analyses. The death benefit comparison [P12], [P13] reveals a £2,038 shortfall (Aviva £65,907 vs existing £67,945), representing 97% of existing benefits. The report does not address whether this loss is offset by the claimed savings or is acceptable. The switching analyses show Nest with 0.30% plan charges plus 1.8% contribution charge; the report's claim that 0.39% Aviva is 'lower' appears to exclude the contribution charge from the comparison, an unacknowledged assumption affecting the validity of the comparison. No aggregated all-in cost illustration is provided, preventing independent verification of cumulative cost impact. The report identifies 'simplicity, cost-efficiency, and flexibility' as benefits but these are qualitative and not measured against the quantified costs or against alternatives. The vulnerability overlay: no recorded vulnerabilities in the evidence affect the cost comparison itself, though poor quantification and opacity about assumptions may impede informed decision-making for any client.

  [G7.8] Reduction in yield, critical yield and cumulative cost illustrations
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report asserts reduction in yield (RIY), critical yield and effect of switching figures for three growth rate scenarios across three products: (1) Aviva at Low (RIY 1.5%, Critical Yield 0.41%, Effect -12.42%), Mid (RIY 1.5%, Critical Yield 3.37%, Effect -12.42%), and High (RIY 1.6%, Critical Yield 6.32%, Effect -12.42%); (2) True Potential (Critical Yield 0.01% showing 0.01% underperformance allowed to age 89); and (3) Nest (Critical Yield 1.23% showing 1.23% outperformance needed to age 89). RIY figures for True Potential and Nest are absent from the report. The report states these figures are from page 21 of the suitability report dated 2026, with ages referenced as 89 (derived from context: client age 55, report to age 89). The report quotes 'Reduction inYield if moved to Aviva 1.5%' (Low and Mid) and 1.6% (High), and 'Rate of Return Required from Aviva' as 0.41% (+0.41%), 3.37% (+0.43%), and 6.32% (+0.44%), with effect of -12.42% across all scenarios.
    File:     Provider illustration [P7] 'Annes M SAP All charges inc existing OAC 24.3.26.md' displays a table with three growth scenarios (0%, 2.94%, 5.88%) showing: RIY for Aviva as 1.5% at all three growth rates; Critical Yield (Rate of Return Required) as 0.41% (+0.41%), 3.37% (+0.43%), and 6.32% (+0.44%); and Effect of Switching as -12.42% at all growth rates. Retirement age shown as 87, not 89. [P6] shows the same table with identical RIY and Critical Yield figures. [P4] shows 'Results Summary' for Aviva with RIY values of 0.5% (not 1.5%) at a single scenario. [P2] shows an alternative illustration without full charges where Aviva RIY is shown as 0.4% (not 1.5%) and Critical Yields differ (0.4% instead of 0.41% for the low scenario). [P10] shows a Nest-only comparison with Reduction in Yield of 0.3% and 0.4%, and Critical Yields of 0.09% (+0.09%), 3.03% (+0.09%), and 5.97% (+0.09%), all with Effect of -2.68% (not -12.42%). All illustrations reference projected ages of 87, not 89.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states retirement age as 89; all provider illustrations show age 87 at proposed retirement
      - Report asserts RIY for Aviva of 1.5% (Low and Mid) and 1.6% (High); illustration [P4] shows RIY of 0.5% for Aviva; illustration [P2] shows RIY of 0.4% for Aviva; neither matches the 1.5%/1.6% asserted
      - Report asserts effect of switching for Aviva as -12.42% consistently; illustration [P10] for Nest shows effect of -2.68%, which is a different product and value
      - Report provides no RIY figure for True Potential or Nest; these are absent from the canonical model
      - Report quotes Critical Yield figures (0.41%, 3.37%, 6.32%) which are corroborated by [P7], but the basis for these differs: report context suggests three separate scenarios, while [P7] shows these as at specified growth rates (0%, 2.94%, 5.88%)
      - RIY mismatch across illustrations: [P2] shows 0.4% RIY with different charges structure (no charges ex existing OAC); [P6]/[P7] show 1.5% RIY with all charges included; [P4] shows 0.5% RIY (IAC only). The charge basis materially affects the RIY but the report does not clearly distinguish which illustration applies to the recommendation
    The core issue is a discrepancy between the retirement age stated in the canonical model (89) and the age shown in all provider illustrations on file (87). The extraction note states '89 derived from context: client currently 55, report states to age 89', but all illustrations in the file reference age 87 explicitly. This 2-year difference is material to RIY calculations as they compound over the projection period.
    
    Second, the RIY figures present a more complex problem. The report asserts 1.5% (Low and Mid) and 1.6% (High) for Aviva; the provider illustration [P7] 'All charges inc existing OAC' corroborates 1.5% at all three growth rates. However, [P4] 'IAC only ex existing OAC' shows 0.5%, and [P2] 'no charges ex existing OAC' shows 0.4%. These are not contradictions in the strict sense—they are the same product under different charge assumptions (as noted in the 'Notes & Assumptions': 'The illustrations take into account the current charging structure'). The report does assert 1.5%, which matches [P6]/[P7], suggesting the 'all charges inc existing OAC' basis. The variation is explained by the guard that illustration growth rates and charge bases differ; RIY at the same basis should be compared. At the 'all charges inc existing OAC' basis, 1.5% is consistent.
    
    However, the third RIY value (1.6% at High growth) presents a minor discrepancy: [P7] shows 1.5% at the highest growth rate (5.88%), not 1.6%. The report asserts 1.6%. This is a 0.1 percentage point difference, which may be rounding or may indicate a different document version not in the pack.
    
    Third, the effect of switching (-12.42%) is corroborated by [P7] for Aviva across all three scenarios. However, [P10] shows effect of -2.68% for Nest, which is a different product and not contradictory. The report correctly distinguishes these by subject.
    
    Fourth, the absence of RIY figures for True Potential and Nest in the report is notable. The report provides Critical Yield figures (0.01% and 1.23%) for these products, which are stated in the suitability report text (per the canonical model provenance), but no RIY is stated for either. The evidence pack does not contain illustrations for True Potential or Nest that would support RIY figures. [P2] shows only Aviva detail; [P10] shows Nest projections with RIY of 0.3% (existing scheme) but no Aviva RIY for comparison in that illustration. The absence of RIY for True Potential and Nest means the recommendation lacks cumulative RIY comparison across the three products being consolidated. This is a disclosure gap.
    
    Fifth, the report's age discrepancy (89 vs 87) affects the validity of all RIY calculations, as RIY is time-dependent. If the report is recommending to age 89 but all supporting illustrations are to age 87, the RIY figures are not directly comparable to what the report asserts.
    
    The Critical Yield figures (0.41%, 3.37%, 6.32%) are arithmetically corroborated by [P7], as is the effect of switching (-12.42%). The three growth rate assumptions (Low, Mid, High) are standard FCA disclosure practice and are applied at 0%, 2.94%, and 5.88% in the illustrations, which aligns with the required format.
    
    No evidence of vulnerability indicators affecting this requirement's assessment has been identified in the evidence pack.

  [G7.9] The service proposition attached to the ongoing adviser charge
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report discloses an ongoing adviser charge of 1% per annum and lists five services included in the ongoing service: (1) Reviewing your attitude to risk and capacity for loss to ensure your investment portfolio remains suitable for you; (2) Reviewing the ongoing suitability of your investment portfolio; (3) Market updates and updates with any changes in financial legislation; (4) Access to a professional, experienced and qualified team of financial advisers at any time to provide ad-hoc advice as required; (5) Access to full holistic financial planning to suit your needs. The report states this charge is applied to fund value per annum and quotes all five service elements from page 26. Review frequency is unspecified in the extracted model.
    File:     The client agreement [P2] states: 'For most investment funds that we transact on your behalf, we charge an ongoing annual review management charge of 1% (subject to a minimum fee of £100), which is automatically taken from the funds invested.' The client agreement [P5] describes 'Ongoing Services' as 'Providing periodic on-going reviews of your existing and/or any new investments we arrange on your behalf to ensure they remain suitable for you.' The client agreement [P7] states that ongoing advice and review services are provided 'as described in the "Important Information about our Services" document we have supplied to you, until either you, or we, consider this service to be no longer appropriate for your needs.' The fee is confirmed in [P2] as 1% per annum matching the report. The client agreement confirms the 1% charge and describes ongoing reviews as core to the service, but does not enumerate the five specific service elements listed in the report.
    Discrepancies:
      - The suitability report lists five specific services included in the 1% ongoing charge (risk review, suitability review, market/legislative updates, access to advisers, holistic financial planning). The client agreement does not itemise these five services; it describes ongoing services only as 'periodic on-going reviews' of investments to ensure suitability and references an 'Important Information about our Services' document that is not provided in the evidence pack.
      - The report specifies that ongoing services include 'Access to a professional, experienced and qualified team of financial advisers at any time to provide ad-hoc advice as required' and 'Access to full holistic financial planning to suit your needs', but the client agreement does not explicitly confirm these elements are included in the 1% charge or describe their scope and availability.
      - Review frequency is stated as 'Unspecified' in the report's extracted model, but no disclosure in either the report or client agreement clarifies how often reviews will occur or on what schedule.
    The 1% per annum charge is consistently stated in both the report and the client agreement [P2]. Both agree the charge is levied on fund value and describe some form of ongoing review service. However, a material discrepancy exists in the scope and specificity of the service proposition. The report discloses five distinct service elements covering risk review, suitability review, market updates, adviser access, and holistic planning. The client agreement acknowledges the 1% charge and periodic reviews but does not itemise or confirm these five specific services are included. The client agreement instead defers detail to an 'Important Information about our Services' document which is not in the evidence pack. The reference to ad-hoc adviser access and holistic financial planning in the report is not echoed in the client agreement's description of the service. This creates ambiguity: either the report is disclosing services beyond what the signed client agreement confirms the client contracted for, or the client agreement's description is incomplete and relies on a missing document. The report fails to cite the minimum frequency of reviews or specify timing, limiting the client's ability to verify the service proposition against actual delivery. The extraction notes that '/ongoingService/reviewFrequency' is 'Unspecified', and no corroborating passage states a review schedule. This is a disclosure shortfall: a 1% ongoing charge warrants explicit statement of review frequency. Additionally, four of the five services listed (all except holistic planning which is marked client-specific) are marked 'isClientSpecific: false', suggesting they are generic terms rather than tailored to this client's circumstances; the report does not explain how these generic services meet this client's specific needs relative to their stated objectives and risk profile.

  [G7.10] Benefits relied upon to justify a higher cost
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The suitability report asserts multiple benefits to justify the recommended switch to Aviva: (1) Lower overall cost structure compared to Quilter — platform charges reduced from 0.27% to 0.19%, saving £208 per annum; (2) Platform charges reduced from 0.40% to 0.19% for True Potential, saving £88.68 per annum; (3) Fund charges reduced from 0.58% to 0.20%, saving £177.35 per annum; (4) Active monitoring and adjustment of investments by adviser; (5) Fund aligned with agreed risk level rather than auto-enrolment design; (6) Access to wider range of investment options (6 funds in Nest vs. broader range in Aviva); (7) More flexible retirement options including tax-efficient withdrawal strategies; (8) Blend of active and passive investments to keep costs low; (9) Online platform for viewing performance, switching funds, and accessing documents; (10) Recommendation based on comprehensive analysis of charges, performance, fund range and retirement options. The report states charges are competitive and provides cost savings of £208 (Quilter), £88.68 (True Potential platform), and £177.35 (True Potential fund charges).
    File:     Evidence from provider documentation and analyses: (1) Aviva platform charges stated as 0.19% in KIIDS document [P11], which matches the report's assertion; (2) Aviva provides flexi-access drawdown with options for phased or single drawdown and tax-efficient income [P6]; (3) Aviva offers Choice — 'Over 6,900 funds and leading managers' [P23] versus Nest's limited fund range; (4) Nest Higher Risk fund documentation [P7, P8] confirms Nest offers only a curated set of funds with specific asset allocations; (5) Switching analysis documents [P1, P3, P4] show projected fund values under no-charge scenarios and comparison between Aviva and existing schemes; (6) Vanguard LifeStrategy 80% Equity Fund within Aviva shows 0.19% ongoing charge [P11]; (7) True Potential existing holdings document [P18] provides performance data for existing funds; (8) Aviva platform provides online access and governance oversight [P5, P23]; (9) SAP analyses [P13, P14, P15] show monthly performance tracking but do not explicitly reconcile the stated annual charge savings. No evidence in the file quantifies the financial benefit of active monitoring or confirms whether the adviser's monitoring service is superior to existing arrangements or worth the implicit cost differential.
    Discrepancies:
      -  Report states £208 annual saving on Quilter platform charges (0.27% to 0.19%), but the basis for this calculation is unclear — no fund value is provided in the report's narrative to verify £208 ÷ 0.08% = £260,000
      - Report states True Potential platform charges save £88.68 (0.40% to 0.19%), implying fund value of £88.68 ÷ 0.21% = £422,285, which does not match True Potential's stated transfer value of £46,672.80 in the switching analysis
      - Report states fund charges reduce from 0.58% to 0.20% saving £177.35, implying fund value of £177.35 ÷ 0.38% = £466,710, which exceeds the True Potential transfer value of £46,672.80
      - Report claims access to wider range of investment options — Nest provides '6 funds' per report but switching analysis [P1, P4] shows Nest offers specifically curated growth, consolidation, and retirement funds; Aviva offers 'Over 6,900 funds' per provider literature [P23] but no evidence that client will select from or benefit from the full range
      - Report asserts 'more flexible retirement options' and 'tax-efficient withdrawal strategies' but True Potential's existing documentation contains no evidence of restricted withdrawal options — no information on True Potential's drawdown flexibility or income options is provided in the file
      - Report states adviser will 'actively monitor and adjust investments as required' but no evidence in the file distinguishes this service from existing arrangements or confirms it was absent under True Potential or Quilter
      - Report references blend of active and passive investments but the specific asset allocation is not detailed; Nest Higher Risk fund [P7, P20] shows its own active-passive blend (e.g., 20% climate-aware global equities, 15% sterling corporate bonds, etc.), so the claimed benefit of Aviva's approach is not substantiated relative to existing holdings
      - No aggregated total cost comparison provided in report — individual platform and fund charge reductions are stated but no cumulative impact or all-in cost for the recommended option is shown
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Current fund value or basis for Quilter platform charge calculation (£208 saving on 0.08% implies £260,000, not stated in report), True Potential fund value and charge reconciliation — £88.68 and £177.35 savings do not reconcile to the stated transfer value of £46,672.80, Nest retirement options documentation — no evidence provided on whether Nest offered drawdown, phased income, or other flexible options prior to switch, True Potential existing withdrawal options and flexibility — no evidence of what drawdown or income features were available, Adviser remuneration structure comparison — no evidence of whether adviser fees or service costs changed between existing arrangements and recommended arrangement, Basis for claimed cost savings — report does not disclose the estimated fund values used to calculate £208, £88.68, and £177.35 annual savings, Performance comparison with controls — SAP analyses show no-charge and with-charge scenarios but report does not clearly state whether performance advantage accrues to Aviva or whether existing funds underperformed, Definition and quantification of active monitoring benefit — no evidence of comparable monitoring under existing arrangements or valuation of this service
    The report asserts cost savings and multiple benefits to justify the recommendation to switch to Aviva. However, several material discrepancies prevent verification. (1) Arithmetic inconsistency on cost savings: The £208 saving on Quilter platform charges (0.08% × fund value) implies a fund value of £260,000, which does not match the canonical model's extraction showing Quilter at £260,000 current value — so this calculation works internally but is not disclosed as the basis. However, the True Potential platform charge saving of £88.68 (0.21% reduction) implies a fund value of £422,285, which far exceeds True Potential's transfer value of £46,672.80. Similarly, the fund charge saving of £177.35 (0.38% reduction) implies a fund value of £466,710, also exceeding True Potential's stated value. These calculations are arithmetically impossible on the stated transfer values and suggest either the basis or the percentages are misstated or misapplied. (2) Benefit substantiation: The report relies on benefits such as 'active monitoring,' 'wider fund range,' 'flexible retirement options,' and 'tax-efficient withdrawal strategies.' Evidence confirms Aviva offers 6,900 funds [P23] and flexi-access drawdown with phased and single withdrawal options [P6]. However, no evidence establishes that Nest or True Potential lacked drawdown or flexible withdrawal options, nor does any document quantify or evidence the value of active adviser monitoring relative to existing arrangements. (3) Fund range claim: Report states Nest 'offers only 6 funds' but the switching analysis [P1, P4] does not specify the exact number of funds available to the client within Nest; literature shows Nest has defined growth, consolidation and retirement funds [P7, P8] with underlying asset allocations, but does not confirm that client access was limited to 6 funds or that this was a material constraint. (4) Consolidated cost comparison absent: The report does not provide an aggregated annual cost figure for the recommended arrangement nor a side-by-side comparison showing total charges (platform + fund + adviser) under all three arrangements. Individual savings are stated but no cumulative impact is shown. (5) Guard: Retirement flexibility is genuinely a benefit if the existing arrangement lacked it, but the file provides no evidence of True Potential's or Nest's existing withdrawal terms, so whether flexibility was already available or represents a net gain cannot be established. (6) Guard: Consolidation benefit — the report does not explicitly claim consolidation; three separate schemes are being consolidated into one Aviva plan, which is a practical benefit but not a financial one and should be weighted as stated. (7) Active monitoring: No evidence distinguishes adviser monitoring under Aviva from that under existing arrangements, nor is any fee or cost associated with this service disclosed or compared.

  [G7.11] Higher charges producing lower projected outcomes or an increased yield requirement
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report states a required outperformance of 1.23% per annum to age 89 when comparing the proposed Aviva plan to the current Nest plan, asserting 'your proposed plan will need to outperform your current plan by 1.23% per annum to age 89 in order to meet the same value as your current plan.' The report also states that the Aviva plan is 'more expensive than your current True Potential plan', requiring only 0.01% underperformance to match value. The report discloses RIY figures of 1.5%, 1.5%, and 1.6% for Aviva across growth scenarios, and critical yield figures of 0.41%, 3.37%, and 6.32% at the 0%, 2.94%, and 5.88% growth rates respectively. A 6.0% mid-level growth rate is assumed in the cashflow analysis. The report recommends Aviva consolidation despite these higher costs.
    File:     The SAP illustrations (provider documents [P3], [P4], [P6], [P7]) corroborate the specific figures: RIY of 1.5% and 1.6% appear in the Nest switch scenarios; critical yield figures of 0.41%, 3.37%, and 6.32% are confirmed in [P4]; the outperformance figure of 1.23% is confirmed in [P2]. No document on file provides an explicit statement of benefits expected to be received by the client in exchange for the higher costs relative to the existing arrangements. The provider illustrations show projected outcomes under different growth scenarios but do not articulate why the client should accept the requirement for 1.23% additional annual outperformance when switching from Nest or why consolidation to Aviva justifies this cost burden.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report asserts a recommended outperformance requirement of 1.23% per annum against Nest but provides no statement of compensating benefits or rationale for accepting this higher-cost proposition; the illustrations show the cost burden but not the stated justification for it.
      - The report states 'more expensive than your current True Potential plan' and notes 0.01% underperformance tolerance, but does not explain why this arrangement is preferable when it is more expensive than at least one existing option.
      - RIY values of 1.5% and 1.6% are disclosed but no comparative cost analysis or cumulative impact table shows the total ongoing adviser remuneration, platform charges, fund charges, and their combined effect over the term to retirement (age 87).
      - The report recommends consolidation to Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) and discloses it is more expensive than existing arrangements but does not quantify or explain in clear terms the benefits (platform efficiency, simplified administration, better fund range, advisory access, or other value drivers) that justify accepting higher costs and critical yield requirements.
    The report correctly identifies and quantifies that the proposed Aviva recommendation requires higher outperformance versus the existing Nest arrangement (1.23% per annum) and is more expensive than the True Potential arrangement. The critical yield and RIY figures are arithmetically consistent with the fund values and charge levels disclosed in the SAP illustrations. However, the report discloses the cost burden without articulating a clear, evidence-supported statement of compensating benefits. The requirement asks whether, where higher costs arise, the report 'clearly explains the benefits expected to be received by the client in exchange for those costs and are they supported by and consistent with the evidence provided.' The report identifies cost increases (RIY 1.5%–1.6%, critical yields 0.41%–6.32%, required outperformance 1.23%) but does not state what benefits justify these costs. The recommendation section notes 'Please refer to the enclosed illustration for a full breakdown of the recommended portfolio' and references investment objectives, attitude to risk, and knowledge/experience, but does not explicitly link any of these to the cost justification. The suitability rationale appears grounded in investment appropriateness (risk profile 7/10, fund selection, age to retirement) rather than explicit cost–benefit analysis. The guard that 'a higher charge buying a materially better fund range or a guarantee may still be justified' does not apply here: the report does highlight the fund range as a PPP benefit in general terms but does not articulate specific, named funds or guarantees obtained through Aviva that the client cannot access through existing cheaper arrangements, nor does it quantify the value of consolidation (administration, reporting, tax efficiency, flexibility benefits). The evidence file contains provider illustrations that show projected outcomes under different growth scenarios but no comparative analysis stating why accepting 1.23% annual outperformance risk is in the client's best interest when lower-cost alternatives exist. This is a disclosure shortfall: the cost comparison is present but the supporting rationale for accepting higher costs is not clearly stated or evidenced.
